# x402 token experiments on Base: PING, x402-mint tokens, launch tools, and the x402-branded token wave (due diligence notes, 2026-10-11)

## Bottom line (research date 2026-10-11)

- **PING is an experiment with no builder to profile.** Anonymous deployer, no team, no product, nothing built since the mint ended on 2025-10-27 (the mint API host is gone from DNS). New on-chain finding: the contract did not mint by itself. An operator-run minter account credited tokens in batches of about 50 to 80 payments, the contract credited exactly 100,000 mints against 138,465 payments counted by x402scan, $100,000 went automatically into the Uniswap pool, and $77,574 was withdrawn on 2025-11-17 by an account the deployer had made an admin minutes earlier. Two accounts still hold the admin role. (Section 1.)
- **PING's template was copied within a week (2025-10-24 to 2025-10-27):** four more contracts reuse its source, one is a factory variant and one is a plain ERC-20 collection box. Across PING and those six, about $603k of USDC left the contracts: 25% into Uniswap pools and about 75% ($453k) withdrawn by admin or owner accounts between 2025-10-25 and 2025-11-17. All seven are dormant. (Section 2.)
- **Launch tools: none has become durable.** x420.dev has been stalled since 2025-11-12; Heurist's X402 Vending Machine made 20 launches in three weeks and nothing since; Covalent's SpeedRun site shows a maintenance notice; x402.team is a meme; APINow.fun has one tokenized API plus its own token; Regents Labs is the only one with heavy 2026 engineering, and x402 is now peripheral to it. I found no x402 mint integration at Clanker or Zora themselves. (Section 2.)
- **Effect on usage numbers:** verified mint payees were about 25% of Base x402 transactions in the week of 2025-10-20 (about 50% counting probable mints), 97% on PING's peak day (2025-10-22), about 1% the week after and about 0% over the last 30 days. The much larger November 2025 surge (64.3M Base transactions in the month) was not mints. (Section 4.)
- **Triage:** CoinGecko's x402 Ecosystem category held 135 coins, 62 with a Base contract; 47 were triaged after exclusions (plus 6 large multi-chain tokens listed separately). Verdicts: 2 Serious builder, 20 Credible but small, 19 Speculative, 5 Likely abandoned, plus TRANSLATE (the token of a profiled product). Most are older AI-agent tokens with an x402 tag: 15 predate x402's launch (2025-05-06) and 15 more predate PING. Apart from AInalyst's click-game endpoint and AnySpend's facilitator, no triaged token's own x402 revenue that I could measure exceeded $1,000. Separately, 127 x402- or 402-named pools on Base were tabulated, 96 of them Clanker deployments. (Section 3.)
- **Narrative hopping:** 63 profiles are scored on the 1-5 rubric: 11 score 5 (the mint family and name-only tokens), 13 score 4, 23 score 3 and 16 score 2. Dated, documented patterns are listed for x420's repo owner, APINow.fun's founder, Capminal, vAPI Network, Otonix, AInalyst, $TRUST ME BROs and Halo among others. Where people are named, the evidence is their own public repos and sites; no pseudonymous person or deployer wallet was linked to a real-world identity. (Section 5.)
- **Limits:** the shared WebSearch budget ran out partway, so most findings come from primary pages and public data APIs; x.com and Telegram were not readable; funding was mostly not found; some sites failed TLS validation from the sub-agents' networks and were not bypassed. Everything not confirmed is labelled in the text.

### Corrections and confirmations of the brief's leads
- PING as the first x402 mint on Base in October 2025: confirmed. The mint started 2025-10-19 and the bulk landed on 2025-10-21 and 2025-10-22, not on 2025-10-23 as several articles say.
- "x402-branded tokens existed before PING": confirmed (a Clanker v3.1 "x402" token on 2025-05-07, others on 2025-08-03 and 2025-10-10; none was an x402 mint).
- PING's site says the contract minted automatically and 100% of the USDC went to locked liquidity; the chain shows an operator-run minter, $100,000 to the pool, and a further $77,574 withdrawn by an admin-role account (section 1).
- The mint tokens do not have "differing bytecodes": BIP, MOOM, x420DOG and MAI share PING's verified source template, and 叉肆零贰 uses a factory variant of it. (An earlier checkpoint of these notes said otherwise.)
- HTTP402's launcher is 0xa52ad458ce0282a971ecc71c051a32f28946bb9f (Blockscout name B20LaunchpadFactory), not 0x826a2b79...; only HTTPBASE20 went through 0x826a2b79... (a fork named Clanker).
- 402.dog (x420DOG) now serves an unrelated page and x402mkt.com now serves Reloop. The sibling note's "x420" row (0x68e284f2...) is a different token from x420DOG (0xd0cb638d...).
- Quick Intel "Tator": the third-party termo.ai listing now returns 404, and its "10% interface fee" and "Flaunch" claims are not in Quick Intel's own sources, which describe Clanker and Pump.fun launches with a 90/10 split of the 1% pool fee.
- Heurist X402 Vending Machine: 20 launches were made (5 graduated), not "unknown"; its docs say "no upgrade keys or admin functions" while the verified contract has an admin role that can withdraw all vault USDC.
- Token Metrics AI [OLD] (TMAI): the x402 tag sits on a legacy Base listing; the company's homepage now points to a Solana mint and shows no x402 text.
- Matching misses in the sibling note's domain-based footprints: Loky (live 402 responses on api.dapplooker.com), WachAI (the 524-transaction figure is payee-level and the payee is shared), MIO (its facilitator footprint sits under MAGIC), AInalyst (the AinX402 endpoint's operator is not confirmed to be the AIN team).
- Covalent: SpeedRun's site shows "Under Maintenance"; Covalent's own repos had 28 commits, all in May 2026 (a June push in its list is an upstream fork).

> Scope note. Date of research: 2026-10-11. "Last six months" = 2026-04-11 to 2026-10-11. Token facts only, no price or chart analysis (market-cap or liquidity figures appear only as liveness indicators). The shared WebSearch budget ran out partway through, so most findings come from direct fetches of primary pages (project sites, docs, GitHub), public data APIs (x402scan's public tRPC endpoint, the Base public RPC, GeckoTerminal, DexScreener, CoinGecko, the Coinbase CDP Bazaar listing, the Clanker public API), and the press articles opened before the cut-off. Anything not confirmed is labelled "unconfirmed" or "not found". On-chain "facts" cite the explorer URL a reader can open; they were read from the Base public RPC (mainnet.base.org) on 2026-10-11. No wallet or pseudonymous person has been linked to a real-world identity; deployer addresses are recorded as contract facts only. In this note "the sub-agent" means one of the helper researchers whose profiles were merged here; their sources are the links in each profile. Projects covered by other researchers (platform layer, token-bearing facilitators, non-token infrastructure, agent frameworks, agent apps and platforms, x402 apps and trust, data and AI sellers, rival chains) are mentioned for context only.

## 1. PING: who built it, what happened next, and is anything still being built?

### Takeaway
PING (Base, contract 0xd85c...9d46) was a real, on-chain, "mint by paying 1 USDC through x402" experiment from 2025-10-19 to 2025-10-27 and the first of a family of x402-mint tokens. It was never a project: its own site says "No utility. No team." and no individual is named anywhere I could open. Nothing has been built since; the mint API host no longer exists in DNS, the static site is still online, and the token still trades (about 127,500 holders, about $0.3M of pool liquidity on 2026-10-11). New on-chain evidence from this pass changes the picture of the mint itself. The payments went to the token contract, but a separate account with a minter role credited the tokens in batches of about 50 to 80 payments per transaction, so the mint depended on an off-chain service rather than running automatically. The contract credited exactly 100,000 mints while x402scan counted 138,465 payments. Of the roughly $177.6k of USDC the contract paid out, $100,000 was deployed to the Uniswap pool at the 100,000th mint (2025-10-22 15:35 UTC) and $77,574 was withdrawn on 2025-11-17 by an account that the deployer had made an admin 3 minutes earlier. Two accounts still hold the admin role today.

### Cited Findings

#### Profile: PING ($PING)
**One-line:** An anonymous memecoin on Base whose supply was "minted" by paying 1 USDC per request through the x402 payment protocol (via the x402scan interface), launched in October 2025 as a protocol "stress test", with no team, roadmap or utility per its own site. [ping.observer](https://ping.observer/) (accessed 2026-10-11)

**x402 role:** Mint mechanism only. The site says: "The entire mint happened via x402scan. 100k transactions at 1 USDC each, all settled through x402", and "Every USDC payment was forwarded directly to a smart contract, which then minted $PING in return." [ping.observer](https://ping.observer/) The x402scan public API (Base, all facilitators) shows the PING token contract itself as the payee of 138,465 payments totalling $138,454 from 19,908 unique payers, first payment 2025-10-19 and last 2025-10-27; 124,095 of them landed on 2025-10-22, which was 97.2% of all x402 transactions that x402scan recorded on Base that day (127,661). [x402scan tRPC `public.stats.bucketed`, recipient filter, queried 2026-10-11](https://www.x402scan.com/api/trpc/public.stats.bucketed) (my own sums of the daily buckets). Press described the user flow as a CLI/URL request, a "402 Payment Required" reply, a $1 USDC payment and a retry, with 5,000 PING per $1 and uncertain success "like inscription minting". [Foresight News via Bitget, 2025-10-24](https://www.bitget.com/news/detail/12560605029396); [Cointelegraph via TradingView](https://www.tradingview.com/news/cointelegraph:ab783ddd6094b:0-why-the-x402-protocol-didn-t-fade-after-the-ping-hype-and-what-s-driving-the-second-wave/)
**How a payment became a mint (new, from the verified source and transactions, read 2026-10-11).** The PING contract is the payee of the x402 payments, but a payment does not mint by itself. The verified source (Sourcify match for `src/Ping.sol`) has `batchMint(address[] to, bytes32[] txHashes)`, callable only by an account holding `MINTER_ROLE`; it records each payment transaction hash in `hasMinted` so a payment cannot be credited twice and mints `mintAmount` (5,000 PING) per hash. [Sourcify source](https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/8453/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46?fields=sources,abi) The deployer granted `MINTER_ROLE` to EOA 0xb828c5Cd...75FD on 2025-10-18 13:00:17 UTC (23 minutes after deployment) and sent that account ETH at 13:01 on 2025-10-18 and twice on 2025-10-21. That account sent the `batchMint` transactions I sampled on 2025-10-22: blocks 37,176,130, 37,176,137, 37,176,145 and 37,176,152, with 70, 55, 78 and 72 mints each, so about one batch every 7 to 8 blocks (roughly every 14 to 16 seconds). [Blockscout transactions](https://base.blockscout.com/address/0xb828c5Cde35f51E4072623e4F5fD9817Fd9d75FD), [Base RPC](https://mainnet.base.org) Both statements in the press are therefore partly right: the site's "USDC in, PING out ... forwarded directly to a smart contract, which then minted $PING in return" omits the off-chain minter, and the Odaily analysis (2025-10-24, citing an X thread by Haotian) was right that an off-chain service decided which payments counted. [Odaily](https://www.odaily.news/en/post/5207078) Odaily's claim that the USDC went to a treasury wallet set by x402scan is not what the chain shows (the payee is the token contract), and I found nothing connecting x402scan to the minter account. I make no claim about who operated the minter. The same source template (with a `batchMint` function, an admin-only `emergencyWithdraw` before the pool is created, `withdrawToken` and `collectLpFees`) appears in the BIP, MOOM, x420DOG and MAI contracts and, in a factory variant, in 叉肆零贰; see section 2.

**Chains:** Base only. [CoinGecko API, category x402-ecosystem, 2026-10-11](https://api.coingecko.com/api/v3/coins/markets?vs_currency=usd&category=x402-ecosystem&per_page=250&page=1)

**Token:** PING; chains: Base; contract 0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46. Deployed 2025-10-18 12:36:53 UTC (block 37,000,833) by externally owned account 0xdbd148a34491c39b7b1a42a539f62f82bbbba43e in a direct contract-creation transaction (not Clanker, not Zora, no factory). [Base explorer: contract](https://basescan.org/address/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46); [creation tx](https://basescan.org/tx/0x221f65c0863e75aaddb112e2c60ed868d5f9530dbdc7a579b7445b3b614b31e1) First x402 payments 2025-10-19 (633 payments from 591 payers: a quiet soft start before the 2025-10-21/22 surge). [x402scan](https://www.x402scan.com/api/trpc/public.stats.bucketed) First Uniswap v4 pool (USDC pair) created 2025-10-22 15:35 UTC. [DexScreener API](https://api.dexscreener.com/latest/dex/tokens/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46) Press dates the launch 2025-10-22 or 2025-10-23. [Bitget/Foresight](https://www.bitget.com/news/detail/12560605029396) Method: "x402 mint" fair launch. On-chain `totalSupply()` = 1,000,000,000 PING (18 decimals), which equals 100,000 mints x 5,000 PING plus the 500,000,000 PING pool seed; `mintCount()` = `maxMintCount()` = 100,000, so no further mint is possible, and `liquidityDeployed()` is true (queried 2026-10-11). [Base explorer](https://basescan.org/address/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46) `owner()` returns the zero address because the deployer called `renounceOwnership` on 2025-10-22 20:57:07 UTC, but that only removes the Ownable owner. The separate AccessControl roles remain: `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` is still held by the deployer EOA and by 0xEc042EA3...33cb (granted by the deployer on 2025-11-10 21:53 UTC), and `MINTER_ROLE` by the deployer and by 0xb828c5Cd...75FD (role checks via `hasRole`, Base RPC, 2026-10-11). The admin role can call `collectLpFees` (collects fees from the pool position; the source has no function that withdraws the pool principal) and `withdrawToken` (sends any ERC-20 held by the contract to the caller). [Sourcify source](https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/8453/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46?fields=sources,abi) The site says "100% of the USDC collected was automatically used to provide liquidity on Uniswap", "locked forever in the contract" and "0% Team Allocation". [ping.observer](https://ping.observer/) Compared with the chain: the pool position is held by the contract and the supply table has no team allocation (true), but only 100,000 USDC (the `paymentSeed`) went to the pool, at 2025-10-22 15:35:17 UTC, and the contract paid out a further 77,574.11 USDC to an admin-role account on 2025-11-17 (see Track record). [ping.observer](https://ping.observer/) Stated utility: none ("No utility. No team. No financial advice."). It does no work in any product because there is no product.

**Team:** None disclosed. FAQ: "No. $PING has no team, no roadmap, no utility, and no allowlist." [ping.observer](https://ping.observer/) Pseudonymous/anonymous. Handles: X @pingobserver, linked from the official site and from OpenSea and GeckoTerminal token info (high confidence it is the project account; no person behind it is named). [ping.observer](https://ping.observer/); [OpenSea](https://opensea.io/token/base/0xd85c31854c2b0fb40aaa9e2fc4da23c21f829d46) GitHub: none linked by the project; at least four look-alike repos that copy the site (for example piraunxx/ping.observer, last pushed 2025-10-25) exist and are not linked from the official site, so treat them as unrelated. [GitHub repo search, 2026-10-11](https://github.com/piraunxx/ping.observer) Telegram: none listed. Farcaster: an account @pingobserver exists (fid 1402447, display name "ping.observer", 8 followers, bio repeating the project's "First coin launched on x402" text); the official site does not link it, so confidence that it is the project's account is moderate (matching handle and text). [Warpcast API](https://api.warpcast.com/v2/user-by-username?username=pingobserver) Some explainers call it an "AI-agent monitoring" project run by "Ping Observer"; the official site says nothing of the kind. [Trust Wallet (search snippet; page not retrievable)](https://trustwallet.com/blog/academy/what-is-x402-and-ping)

**Track record and pivots:** No pivots; one event. 2025-10-18 contract deployed. 2025-10-19 first x402 payments. 2025-10-21/22 mass mint (13,715 and 124,095 payments). 2025-10-22 15:35 UTC first Uniswap v4 pool. 2025-10-24 x402scan seven-day figures: 163,600 transactions (+701.7%), about $140,200 volume, about 31,000 buyers. [Coinfomania via cryptonews](https://cryptonews.net/news/altcoins/31852534/) 2025-10-25 peak: market cap briefly above $57M [Jinse via Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/news/detail/12560605057035); all-time high about $0.0776 [Cointelegraph via TradingView](https://www.tradingview.com/news/cointelegraph:ab783ddd6094b:0-why-the-x402-protocol-didn-t-fade-after-the-ping-hype-and-what-s-driving-the-second-wave/). 2025-10-27 market cap below $30M. [Jinse via Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/news/detail/12560605057035) 2026-06-03 Chainalysis: PING-driven wallets showed 87% weekly retention for one week, then 5% as they went dormant. [Chainalysis](https://www.chainalysis.com/blog/x402-agentic-payments-adoption/) 2026-10-11: `api.ping.observer` returns NXDOMAIN in Google DNS (the mint server host is gone; the date of removal is unknown) while the apex site still serves HTTP 200. [Google DNS lookup](https://dns.google/resolve?name=api.ping.observer&type=A)

Dated on-chain events from the deployer and contract histories (Blockscout address and transaction API, read 2026-10-11; addresses are recorded as contract facts only): 2025-10-18 12:36:53 deployment; 13:00:17 deployer grants `MINTER_ROLE` to 0xb828c5Cd...75FD; 2025-10-22 15:35:17 the 100,000th mint triggers pool creation and a 100,000 USDC transfer into the Uniswap v4 pool manager; 2025-10-22 15:48 to 2025-10-24 08:25 the deployer calls `collectLpFees` eight times (15:48, 18:39, 19:50, 20:09 and 20:57 on 10-22, 10:21 and 17:29 on 10-23, 08:25 on 10-24); 2025-10-22 20:57:07 deployer renounces Ownable ownership; 2025-11-10 21:53:51 deployer grants `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` to 0xEc042EA3...33cb; 2025-11-17 10:32:37 deployer grants `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` to 0x88CaB199...FD12; 10:35:21 and 10:36:57 that account calls `withdrawToken` and takes 1 USDC, then 77,573.114567 USDC, from the contract to itself; 10:50:03 it renounces the role; it makes further USDC transfers on 2025-11-17, 2025-11-28 and 2026-01-08/12 (destinations not traced). [Blockscout: contract USDC transfers](https://base.blockscout.com/api/v2/addresses/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46/token-transfers?type=ERC-20&filter=from), [deployer transactions](https://base.blockscout.com/api/v2/addresses/0xdbd148A34491C39b7b1a42A539f62F82BbbbA43E/transactions?filter=from), [admin-role account transactions](https://base.blockscout.com/api/v2/addresses/0x88CaB199Be21521D8aFc9C112C41d05A34CfFD12/transactions?filter=from)

**Trend-hopping score:** 5 on the rubric's own terms (narrative-only, no product), with a caveat: there is no persistent team, so no documented hopping between narratives. It was a one-off riding the x402 launch narrative.

**Funding:** None disclosed; no investors named. [Cointelegraph via TradingView](https://www.tradingview.com/news/cointelegraph:ab783ddd6094b:0-why-the-x402-protocol-didn-t-fade-after-the-ping-hype-and-what-s-driving-the-second-wave/) The site says mint proceeds seeded the liquidity (self-claim).

**Product and traction:** No product. Traction was the mint itself: x402scan counts 138,465 payments, $138,454, 19,908 unique payers (about 7 payments per payer, which points to scripted repeat minting; Chainalysis also notes near-zero Base gas allowed repeat loops). [x402scan](https://www.x402scan.com/api/trpc/public.stats.bucketed); [Chainalysis](https://www.chainalysis.com/blog/x402-agentic-payments-adoption/) The site calls the target "100,000 mint transactions". The contract stops crediting at its cap of 100,000, so the 38,465 payments x402scan counted beyond that number (about $38.5k at $1 each) had no mint to match; I found no refund among the contract's three USDC transfers out. The contract paid out 177,574.11 USDC in total (100,000 to the pool, 77,574.11 withdrawn) and holds 0 now, which implies more USDC reached it than the $138,454 x402scan counts; the roughly $39k gap is unexplained (x402scan may not index every facilitator that settled payments). GeckoTerminal lists 127,553 holders (2026-10-10), FDV about $0.46M, pool liquidity about $257k, 24h volume under $5k on 2026-10-11, and files it under its "X402 Protocol" category. [GeckoTerminal API](https://api.geckoterminal.com/api/v2/networks/base/tokens/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46/info) CoinGecko ranks it about #3,790 in its x402 category snapshot. [CoinGecko API](https://api.coingecko.com/api/v3/coins/markets?vs_currency=usd&category=x402-ecosystem&per_page=250&page=1)

**Last six months (2026-04-11 to 2026-10-11):** Nothing shipped. No GitHub, no announcements found, mint API host removed from DNS, static site still live. The token is still tradable on Uniswap v4 and later appeared as a quote asset in a few tiny pools (SPAWN/PING and NOT/PING in 2025-12). [GeckoTerminal search, 2026-10-11](https://api.geckoterminal.com/api/v2/search/pools?query=ping%20x402&network=base)

**Direction:** None stated ("no roadmap"). Its lasting effect was as a template and a stimulus: Cointelegraph argues the lasting impact was the influx of developers and endpoints experimenting with x402, not the token price, and Chainalysis says the Q4 2025 weekly surge (over 10,000% in one week) was "driven largely by meme coin activity, especially PING". [Cointelegraph via TradingView](https://www.tradingview.com/news/cointelegraph:ab783ddd6094b:0-why-the-x402-protocol-didn-t-fade-after-the-ping-hype-and-what-s-driving-the-second-wave/); [Chainalysis](https://www.chainalysis.com/blog/x402-agentic-payments-adoption/) (Section 4 shows PING itself was only a few days of that surge.)

**Competitors:** Other x402-mint tokens from the same week (BIP, x402 [0x4c15], x420DOG, MAI and others; see section 2) and the 130-plus x402-named Clanker tokens that followed (section 3). None is a competitor in a product sense.

**Green and red flags:** Green: fixed 1B supply that matches the mint arithmetic; the cap of 100,000 mints holds and no further mint is possible; the pool position is owned by the contract and the source has no function to withdraw its principal; Ownable ownership renounced; candid "no team, no utility" copy. Red: anonymous; the mint depended on one off-chain minter account that the deployer funded and authorised; two accounts still hold `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE`, which can collect pool fees (the deployer did so eight times in the first two days) and sweep any ERC-20 held by the contract; 77,574 USDC was withdrawn by an admin-role account on 2025-11-17, which the site's "100% of the USDC collected" wording does not mention; 38,465 payments exceeded the 100,000 mints that were credited; scripted repeat minting (about 7 payments per payer); look-alike GitHub copies; some explainers mislabel it as an AI-agent project; mint API host now gone; peak-to-now collapse of a token that was sold on x402 narrative alone. Not confirmed: whether the 77,574 USDC covered refunds, costs or profit (the withdrawal went to the caller's own account and onward transfers were not traced).

**Verdict:** Speculative (an experiment, not a project). Momentum: Dead as a project; the token merely persists. Opinion: PING deserves a place in the x402 story as the first public "mint by paying through x402" event and as the thing that made x402scan's counters jump, but it is not a builder and has no direction. The checkable parts of its design held up (fixed supply, protocol-owned pool, renounced Ownable owner). The parts its site left out matter more for due diligence: minting ran through a privileged off-chain account, the admin keys were never given up, and about 44% of the USDC that passed through the contract ended up with an admin-role account instead of in the pool. What it proved is that anyone can point x402 at a token contract and run a mint service behind it, and that most of the resulting "transactions" were repeat mints rather than API payments.

**Tracking card:** Website https://ping.observer · Docs: none · GitHub: none official · X: @pingobserver (project; linked from the official site) · people: none named · Farcaster: @pingobserver (fid 1402447; found via the Warpcast API, not linked from the site, moderate confidence) · Telegram: none · Token contract (Base): 0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46 · Deployer EOA (contract fact only): 0xdbd148a34491c39b7b1a42a539f62f82bbbba43e · minter-role EOA: 0xb828c5cde35f51e4072623e4f5fd9817fd9d75fd · other admin-role holder: 0xec042ea35e41834995c919d9c5e46f7a9c4c33cb · account that withdrew on 2025-11-17 (role since renounced): 0x88cab199be21521d8afc9c112c41d05a34cffd12. No person is linked to any of these addresses.

#### Corrections to the brief (PING)
- "First token minted through x402 payments on Base, October 2025": confirmed. The mint started 2025-10-19 (x402scan data), bulk on 2025-10-21/22, not on 2025-10-23 as several articles say. [x402scan](https://www.x402scan.com/api/trpc/public.stats.bucketed)
- "x402-branded tokens existed before PING": an "x402" token was deployed through Clanker v3.1 on 2025-05-07 00:23 UTC (the day after x402 launched on 2025-05-06, per [Jinse via Bitget](https://www.bitget.com/news/detail/12560605057035)) and another on 2025-08-03; a third, "X402", on 2025-10-10. None was an x402 mint. [Base explorer: 0xb2dd89d9…9b07](https://basescan.org/address/0xb2dd89d9a5c97880880ee054725131acd03f9b07); [0x6064cb5c…fb07](https://basescan.org/address/0x6064cb5c1f80909aea3fe20278896f235e49fb07); [0x8c742d92…db07](https://basescan.org/address/0x8c742d92c0111edcc31828ce9ff8e79e76c6db07)

### Inferences
- PING mattered as a catalyst, not as a project: it exposed x402 to a speculative audience in one week, then left no team, product or channel to carry that attention.
- The 138,465 payments against 100,000 credited mints, and about 7 payments per payer, suggest bot-driven minting and a late rush after the cap: payers after the 100,000th mint paid $1 and got nothing from the contract. That, not organic API demand, is what PING contributed to x402 counters.
- The mint was a service, not a protocol feature: a contract that is the payee plus an operator-run minter that reads settled payment hashes. That makes "x402 mint" a template anyone can copy, which the next week's tokens did (section 2).
- The "AI-agent monitoring project" description in some explainers appears to be a misreading of the pseudonymous account name "pingobserver".

### Gaps
- Who operated the minter account and the admin accounts: not documented, and I did not try to identify anyone. The Odaily thread's link between x402scan and the mint server is unconfirmed.
- Where the 77,574 USDC went after 2025-11-17 and what it paid for (refunds, costs or profit); the account's later USDC transfers were not traced.
- Whether payers beyond the 100,000th mint were refunded from another account.
- Why x402scan's 138,465 payments ($138,454) is lower than the roughly $177.6k the contract paid out.
- Activity of the @pingobserver X account since 2025: x.com is not fetchable; unconfirmed.
- Whether the Trust Wallet explainer's "Ping Observer monitors AI agents" claim has any basis: the page could not be retrieved (only the search snippet), unconfirmed.

## 2. Which tools let anyone mint or launch tokens through x402 payments, who built them, and are they serial token launchers?

### Takeaway
Three kinds of tool exist on Base and none has become a durable product. (1) The PING mint template: a token contract that is the x402 payee, with an operator-run minter account crediting a fixed token amount per payment hash and an admin who can withdraw the USDC. Six contracts used it or a factory variant (and a seventh was a plain ERC-20 collection box) between 2025-10-19 and 2025-10-27; all seven are dormant, and operators withdrew about 75% of the USDC that passed through them. (2) Launch-as-a-service sites that take an x402 payment and deploy through Clanker or their own ERC-20s: x420.dev (stalled since 2025-11-12), Heurist's X402 Vending Machine (20 launches between 2025-10-28 and 2025-11-14, none since), Covalent SpeedRun (site in maintenance), APINow.fun (one tokenized API plus a platform token), x402MKT/Reloop (rebranded) and x402.team (a meme). Regents Labs, whose Autolaunch runs token auctions, is the only one with heavy 2026 engineering, and x402 is now peripheral to it. (3) Copies on other chains (Hello 402 on X Layer, XFOUR on BNB Chain, X402.Fun on Solana devnet, 1ly's Bags.fm launches on Solana; MNTx402 unconfirmed). I found no x402 mint integration at Clanker or Zora themselves: Clanker is the plumbing behind most tools, and Zora's only x402 feature is a buyer-side `zora pay` command. Serial launching exists mainly as shared launch wallets (0xd9acd656... 307 Clanker tokens, 0x2112b845... 113, 0x0949ed2c... 4,738), which I treat as launch services rather than teams, and a few named builders (APINow.fun's founder, whose public wallet role covers 66 Clanker tokens, and the MiroShark builder with two tokens in two weeks). [sibling note: dd_agent_apps_platforms.md](dd_agent_apps_platforms.md)

### Cited Findings

#### Tools and templates found (summary table)
Status is as of 2026-10-11. Profiles follow below; "x402scan" figures come from its public API.

| Tool | Chain and mechanism | Built by (verified how) | Evidence of use | Status | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PING mint template (PING, BIP, MOOM, x420DOG, MAI; factory variant 叉肆零贰) | Base; token contract is the x402 payee; a minter-role account calls `batchMint` per payment hash; automatic Uniswap v4 pool at the cap; admin `withdrawToken` | Anonymous; deployer and minter wallets are contract facts only | $553k across seven token-contract payees (2025-10-19 to 2025-11-28) | All dormant; about 75% of USDC withdrawn by operators | Speculative; Dead |
| x402.team token | Base; Clanker token plus a 1 USDC mint contract (500,000 tokens per mint; owner can change fee address) | Anonymous | Mint count not found | Site live; volume under $10 a day | Speculative; Dead |
| x420.dev | Base; $5 deploy fee, Clanker liquidity, 78 tokens listed | Pseudonymous repo owner (GitHub markeljan) | 144,840 payments / $31,225 (2025-10-23 to 11-12) | Stalled; last commit 2026-03-25 | Speculative; Stalled |
| Heurist X402 Vending Machine | Base; x402 USDC buys locked tokens in a 14-day sale, then a HEU pair | Heurist (established; commits by one account) | 20 launches, 5 graduated; 105,587 payments / $43,310 | Dormant since 2026-01-16; front end 522 | Likely abandoned; Stalled |
| Covalent SpeedRun | Base; prompt-to-app builder with Clanker tokens and x402 payments (announced 2025-10-30) | Covalent (named team, since 2017) | No usage numbers found | Site shows "Under Maintenance" | Likely abandoned (SpeedRun) |
| APINow.fun | Base; x402 pay-per-call API marketplace, API tokens via Clanker, Tokenback | Chris Dolinski (self-declared, public repos) | 14,070 payments / $228 all-time; one tokenized API (TRANSLATE) | Slowing; $2 in 2026-10 so far | Credible but small; Slowing |
| Regents Labs Autolaunch | Base and Robinhood Chain; token auctions; x402 only in Patchbay | Sean Brennan (About page, GitHub, Farcaster) | $8,171 USDC into staking in 5 months | Heavy commits through 2026-10-11 | Credible but small; Accelerating |
| Quick Intel Tator | Base and Solana; Tator agent can launch on Clanker and Pump.fun (skills repo); x402 gateway at $0.20 a call | Company; no individuals named | No usage data | Gateway healthy (v1.5.0) | Credible but small; Steady |
| x402MKT / Reloop | Base; pooled Clanker launches with x402 USDC contributions | Anonymous | No x402 footprint found | Domain now serves Reloop | Speculative; Stalled |
| Treasure AI Frens / Summon | Base; x402-compatible AI-character token launcher (listed 2025-11-11) | Treasure (established) | Facilitator 2,461 tx / $400 all-time | Summon beta live | See MIO profile |
| TokenMesa | Listed 2025-12-03 as service-backed tokens on x402 | Unknown | None found | Site is an empty redirect; unverified | Likely abandoned (unverified) |

#### Mint-template family: mechanism and where the USDC went
All numbers read on 2026-10-11 from the verified sources (Sourcify), Base RPC calls and the Blockscout token-transfer API for each contract. A withdrawal is not proof of wrongdoing, and I found no refund transfers; destinations after the first hop were not traced.

| Token | Template | Mint cap / credited | Pool at the cap | USDC withdrawn by admin or owner | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PING | original | 100,000 / 100,000 | yes: 100,000 USDC, 2025-10-22 15:35 UTC | 77,574 on 2025-11-17 | separate minter EOA funded by the deployer; two admin-role accounts remain |
| BIP | PING template | 100,000 / 99,999 | no (one mint short) | 122,287 (2025-10-26 to 10-29) | `emergencyWithdraw` on 2025-10-26 10:39 UTC also minted 500M BIP to the deployer |
| MOOM | template, no seed | 50,000 / 9,359 | none by design | 11,159 on 2025-10-29 | deployer is owner and minter; holds 98.2% of supply |
| x420DOG | PING template | 10,000 / 10,000 | yes: 50,000 USDC, 2025-10-24 19:25 UTC | 75,998 on 2025-11-10 | 75,871 payments for 10,000 mints |
| MAI | PING template | 10,000 / 9,731 | no | 101,190 on 2025-11-04 | deployer is owner and minter |
| 叉肆零贰 | factory variant (Launchpad402) | 100,000 / about 23,539 | none found | 55,225 (2025-10-27 and 11-04) | per-launch owners; tied to 402rush.fun |
| x402 (0x4c15...) | plain ERC-20, no mint logic | n/a | no | 10,034 (2025-10-25 and 10-26) | owner-only `withdrawAllERC20` |

Totals: about $603,468 of USDC left these contracts; $150,000 (25%) went into pools and about $453,468 (75%) was withdrawn by admin or owner accounts. x402scan counts $553k of payments to the seven payees, so the outflows exceed what it counts by about $50k. [x402scan public API](https://www.x402scan.com/api/trpc/public.sellers.all.list), [Sourcify](https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/8453/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46?fields=sources,abi), [Blockscout API](https://base.blockscout.com/api/v2/addresses/0xd85c31854c2B0Fb40aaA9E2Fc4Da23C21f829d46/token-transfers?type=ERC-20&filter=from) For a non-technical reader: "x402 mint" here meant paying $1 to a token's contract address, then waiting for the operator's server to read the payment and send tokens; the operator also held the keys to the USDC.

#### Clanker, Zora, Bankr and B20 launchers
- Clanker: 96 of the 127 x402- or 402-named pools tabulated in section 3D were Clanker deployments; the tools above (x420, x402.team, APINow.fun, Regents, SpeedRun, x402MKT, Tator) deploy through Clanker v4 or v3.1 factories rather than through any x402 feature of Clanker. A sibling researcher's code search found no material x402 work in the Clanker organisation. [sibling note](dd_agent_apps_platforms.md)
- Zora: its only x402 feature found is the buyer-side `zora pay` CLI command (v1.6.0, 2026-06-25); no mint or launch integration. [sibling note](dd_agent_apps_platforms.md)
- Bankr: some x402-named tokens were launched through Bankr-style ERC-4337 deploys (Hermes OS 2026-04-13, LUCA 2026-05-16, x402 Identity 2026-09-26, GMCLAW 2026-02-05); HTTPBASE20's profile text says it was created by an x402 call from the Bankr agent (a self-description). Bankr itself is covered by another researcher.
- B20 launchers: the B20LaunchpadFactory (verified 2026-07-11) created HTTP402 on 2026-07-26 and a Clanker-named fork created HTTPBASE20 on 2026-07-16; both carry x402 names and no product.
- Other tools in the one-liners below: Pantessa/Yeetful's token-per-MCP launchpad (Flaunch fork, testnet only), a Virtuals "Token Launch Agent" registered in ERC-8004 on 2026-02-04, and a sniping script for Base x402 launches (repo 0xwenjian/x402_script, 2026-07-29).

#### Serial-launcher evidence
- Shared wallets, not teams: 0xd9acd656... sent 307 Clanker deployments (2025-03 to 2026-07), including TRANSLATE (2025-05-29), x420 (2025-10-23) and GMCLAW (2026-02-05) and described on GMCLAW's page as a Bankr deploy; 0x2112b845... sent 113 (mostly 2026-01 and 2026-02, plus HEIR 2025-03-23 and StarkBot 2026-01-28); 0x0949ed2c... sent 4,738. These look like launch services used by many people. [Clanker API](https://www.clanker.world/api/tokens/fetch-deployed-by-address?address=0xd9acd656a5f1b519c9e76a2a6092265a74186e58)
- Named builders with several launches: APINow.fun's founder (TRANSLATE 2025-05-29, GMCLAW 2026-02-05, VINI 2026-02-13, $APINOW 2026-02-25; the wallet Clanker shows as $APINOW's creator is admin of 66 Clanker tokens, with fee shares on recent ones going to other addresses, which looks like launching for others); the MiroShark builder ($AEON, then $MIROSHARK two weeks later); Regents Labs (REGENT, then Autolaunch auctions that list other teams' tokens).
- Mint-family operators: seven contracts, each with a deployer or owner that withdrew funds within 3 to 27 days of deployment; whether any deployer is the same person is not established and was not investigated.

#### Profiles (tools and templates)
Each profile follows the requested headings. Opinions are labelled as opinion; people are recorded only from official pages, self-declared profiles or the project's own links.

#### Profile: Regents Labs ($REGENT)
**One-line:** A one-founder company (Regents Labs, Inc.) that launched the Base token REGENT in Nov 2025 as an "agent x402 revshare" coin. It now runs Autolaunch (token auctions), Techtree (agent Skill evaluations), Patchbay (a help board for agents) and Keyfleet (not open), all tied to REGENT staking. [About](https://regents.sh/about), [Home](https://www.regents.sh)
**x402 role:**
- The launch pitch was x402-centred. The Clanker description (deployed 2025-11-06) reads "agent x402 revshare, tokenized", and the launch cast said agents would get money through x402, issue their own coin and build onchain reputation. [Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07), [launch cast](https://farcaster.xyz/regent/0xff499bbc)
- Today x402 is a side feature. Patchbay takes optional USDC on Base: paid priority reports, and 0.10 USDC paid fixes after two free per wallet per 24 hours. Callers bring their own x402 signature, and regents-cli says it never signs a payment. [Patchbay llms.txt](https://patchbay.help/llms.txt), [regents-cli](https://github.com/regents-ai/regents-cli)
- The Autolaunch README and the Techtree page do not mention x402. [Autolaunch README](https://github.com/regents-ai/autolaunch), [techtree.sh](https://techtree.sh)
- Cross-check: the sibling table (row 306) matched no x402 footprint for Regent by domain. x402scan indexes only registered facilitators, so this does not prove zero use. [sibling note](ecosystem_map_and_metrics.md)

**Chains:** Base (token, staking, Revstake auctions, USDC payments) and Robinhood Chain (Memestake auctions). regents-cli can list an agent in the ERC-8004 registry on Base. [About](https://regents.sh/about), [Autolaunch README](https://github.com/regents-ai/autolaunch), [regents-cli](https://github.com/regents-ai/regents-cli)
**Token:**
- REGENT, Base 0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07. [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/token/0x6f89bca4ea5931edfcb09786267b251dee752b07)
- Created 2025-11-06 16:01:07 UTC through the Clanker v4 factory (Base RPC read by the sub-agent). The Clanker page labels the interface "Clanker SDK" and the deployer @seanwbren; the creation tx was sent by 0x0cb27e88...3c37, which that page lists as admin and reward recipient. [Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07)
- Supply 100B: 20% pool, 40% vault (unlocks 2026-11-06, fully vested 2028-11-05) and 40% airdrop, whose single allocation went to the deployer's own wallet. Dev buy 4.6475 ETH. On 2026-10-11 there were 2,357 holders and the top ten held 93.62%. [Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07)
- Stated utility: staking pays a pro-rata share of USDC deposits plus REGENT emissions at 20% APR (the contract maximum); REGENT is also the bid currency for Revstake auctions. [Stake page](https://regents.sh/stake), [Autolaunch README](https://github.com/regents-ai/autolaunch), [Sourcify ABI](https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/8453/0xb027Dc261636E30Cbc0fE25b2F8e1ed273354AB5?fields=abi)
- Does real work, with small money. The staking contract (deployed 2026-05-05) had received 8,171.164 USDC in total, credited 563.11 USDC to stakers and held 9.0046B REGENT staked (Base RPC reads at block 52,465,788). [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0xb027Dc261636E30Cbc0fE25b2F8e1ed273354AB5)
- Liveness: still trading, Uniswap v4 liquidity about $144k, 24h volume under $200 (2026-10-11). [DexScreener](https://dexscreener.com/base/0x4ed3b69ac263ad86482f609b2c2105f64bcfd3a7e02e8e078ec9fec1f0324bed)

[Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07), [Farcaster @seanwbren](https://api.warpcast.com/v2/user-by-username?username=seanwbren), [Farcaster @regent](https://api.warpcast.com/v2/user-by-username?username=regent)

[llms.txt](https://regents.sh/llms.txt), [regents README](https://github.com/regents-ai/regents)

**Last six months (2026-04-11 to 2026-10-11):** GitHub commits by calendar month (Apr, May, Jun, Jul, Aug, Sep, Oct 1-11; April covers Apr 1-10): regents 70/28/41/91/234/330/90; regents-cli 70/28/41/25/8/88/53; autolaunch 0/0/0/0/55/649/125; techtree 0/0/0/0/501/337/101; patchbay 0/0/0/0/0/411/103 ([org](https://github.com/regents-ai)). Shipped: staking contract (2026-05-05), Autolaunch v2 (2026-09-24), API and docs change (2026-09-28). [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0xb027Dc261636E30Cbc0fE25b2F8e1ed273354AB5), [docs](https://regents.sh/docs)
**Direction:** The pages name Keyfleet as opening soon, Regents Mobile (iPhone wallet) as not out, Techtree environments and NeMo support as planned, and a Robinhood-to-Base bridge as not built. The vault unlock starts 2026-11-06. [About](https://regents.sh/about), [Autolaunch README](https://github.com/regents-ai/autolaunch), [Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07)
**Competitors:** My categories, unsourced: Clanker, Bankr and Virtuals for token launches; other auction-style launchers; agent-evaluation hubs (Techtree uses Prime Intellect's verifiers). I found no direct rival to Patchbay.
**Green and red flags:**
- Green: named founder with a consistent identity across official pages, GitHub and Clanker; public MIT code with heavy ongoing commits; staking source verified, with on-chain totals matching the site; the Autolaunch README states there is no outside audit. [Autolaunch README](https://github.com/regents-ai/autolaunch)
- Red: at launch the deployer controlled 80% of supply and the top ten holders hold 93.62% [Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07); the staking owner is a contract (171 bytes) whose ABI shows it can pause, change the emission rate up to 20% APR, sweep the reward pool and rescue tokens [Sourcify ABI](https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/8453/0xb027Dc261636E30Cbc0fE25b2F8e1ed273354AB5?fields=abi); revenue is small (about $8.2k deposited in 5 months, $563 credited to stakers); most code is written with an AI co-author; the product line has changed repeatedly.
- Not confirmed: whether the 40% deployer-wallet allocation reached outside holders; the Stake page shows 20.83B in the treasury. [Stake page](https://regents.sh/stake)

**Verdict:** Credible but small; momentum Accelerating. Opinion: one founder is shipping fast with AI help, and the contracts and staking page are open and consistent. Real revenue is about $8k in five months, almost all supply sat with the deployer at launch, and x402 is now peripheral. The direction has moved four times in a year. Watch the 2026-11-06 vault unlock and whether Autolaunch v2 attracts outside projects.
**Tracking card:** Website regents.sh; products autolaunch.sh, techtree.sh, patchbay.help, keyfleet.ai, template.regents.sh, siwa.regents.sh · docs regents.sh/docs, regents.sh/llms.txt, regents.sh/openapi.json · GitHub regents-ai (org), sean-regents · X @regents_sh (project), @seanwbren (founder) · Farcaster @regent (FID 1399664), @seanwbren (FID 2139) · Telegram not found · token 0x6f89bcA4eA5931EdFCB09786267b251DeE752b07 · staking 0xb027Dc261636E30Cbc0fE25b2F8e1ed273354AB5

#### Profile: x420 (x420.dev)
**One-line:** A launchpad site where you pay about $5 to deploy a token with its own subdomain and Clanker liquidity. It also hosts a joke whitepaper on rate limiting (HTTP 420, "Calm Your Requests"). [x420.dev](https://x420.dev)
**x402 role:**
- The site shows a $5 price for an x402 token and API but does not say in text that the $5 is paid through x402. The Clanker description says the token was launched on x402. [x420.dev](https://x420.dev), [Clanker x420](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x68e284F21D04D4D62398C290EC3EF41C97F80b07)
- Repo commits on 2025-10-24 add an x402 payment middleware, a Coinbase facilitator setting and an API route for buying x420 tokens. [commits](https://github.com/markeljan/x420/commits/main)
- The x402scan payee linked from the site is 0x93109d...bafdbd. The x402scan pull made for this note shows 144,840 payments worth $31,225.30 from 2,154 buyers, 2025-10-23 to 2025-11-12 (21 active days), and none after. [x402scan payee](https://www.x402scan.com/recipient/0x93109d14f6665a9c9675290d3cd5a6c07bbafdbd)
- Shape of that traffic: 2025-10-24 alone had 9,451 payments, $18,867 and 1,702 buyers. November had 122,056 payments worth $136.80, about $0.001 each, which looks automated (inference).
- Cross-check, sibling table row 322: origin x8004.x420.dev, 144.8K tx / $31.2K, matching this pull. Flag: x8004 is one of the 78 listed tokens, so these payments may belong to that token's API rather than the $5 deploy fee. [sibling note](ecosystem_map_and_metrics.md)
- The payee is a 61-byte ERC-1967-style proxy contract (nonce 1) pointing to a 17.7 KB implementation the sub-agent did not identify (Base RPC read). [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0x93109d14f6665a9c9675290d3cd5a6c07bbafdbd)

**Chains:** Base. [Clanker x420](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x68e284F21D04D4D62398C290EC3EF41C97F80b07)
**Token:**
- x420, Base 0x68e284F21D04D4D62398C290EC3EF41C97F80b07. [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/token/0x68e284f21d04d4d62398c290ec3ef41c97f80b07)
- Created 2025-10-23 18:12:59 UTC through the Clanker v4 factory (Base RPC read). The Clanker page shows interface clanker.world, platform Farcaster, and creator @soko.eth. [Clanker x420](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x68e284F21D04D4D62398C290EC3EF41C97F80b07)
- The creation tx came from 0xd9acd656..., an account with no code and nonce 422,604, which makes it a high-volume shared sender, not a team wallet. The sibling note says it has 307 Clanker deployments; that count was not verified. [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0xd9acd656a5f1b519c9e76a2a6092265a74186e58)
- The description says the token has no utility. It does no real work in the product.
- Liveness: Uniswap v4 liquidity about $146k, 24h volume about $182 (2026-10-11). [DexScreener](https://dexscreener.com/base/0x9461ebccb508dcde49e54073bb587fb78e44ccdbcdbfe113c7c77d112e12c370)

**Team:**
- Pseudonymous; the site names no one. The site links X @x420yo. [x420.dev](https://x420.dev)
- GitHub markeljan owns the repo, whose homepage is x420.dev. The profile self-declares company @semiotic-ai, blog w3gpt.ai and X soko_eth, and the account dates from 2015-06-15. [profile](https://github.com/markeljan)
- A commit by markeljan at 2025-10-23 18:14 UTC adds the token's contract address, one minute after creation, which makes markeljan the repo owner and token operator with high confidence. [commits](https://github.com/markeljan/x420/commits/main)
- The Clanker creator is Farcaster soko.eth. The match to X soko_eth is by name only (medium confidence). Whether either is the @x420yo account is not confirmed. [Clanker x420](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0x68e284F21D04D4D62398C290EC3EF41C97F80b07)
- GitHub microchipgnu co-wrote commits on 2025-10-23 to 25 through a separate x420-website repo. [commits](https://github.com/markeljan/x420/commits/main)
- No real names recorded.

**Track record and pivots:**
- The documented repo history shows a new theme every few months: 2024-01 a Stacks token relayer; 2024-03 an sOrdinals NFT wrapper; 2024-12 to 2025-04 Bitte agent repos; 2025-06 a Supra game; 2025-08 a Coinbase hackathon app (L00T.FUN) and a fork of the MCPay x402 payments project; 2025-10-25 x420; 2026-02 an AI shell CLI; 2026-04 forks of OpenClaw. [repos](https://github.com/markeljan?tab=repositories)
- x420 timeline: token 2025-10-23; repo created 2025-10-25; last x402 payment 2025-11-12; last commit 2026-03-25 (a README for a template). [repo](https://github.com/markeljan/x420)

**Trend-hopping score (1-5):** 4. The launchpad was built within a day of the PING wave and sits on a long run of short projects that follow each new narrative. It does have working code (38 commits), which keeps it below 5. This rests on the repo owner only, since the link to the X account is unconfirmed. [repo](https://github.com/markeljan/x420), [repos](https://github.com/markeljan?tab=repositories)
**Funding:** Not found.
**Product and traction:** The site lists 78 tokens (2026-10-11), with no dates; many names look like tests, and the top card is x420 itself. x402 traffic stopped in mid-Nov 2025 (above). Repo: 0 stars, 3 forks. [x420.dev](https://x420.dev), [repo](https://github.com/markeljan/x420)
**Last six months (2026-04-11 to 2026-10-11):** 0 commits to x420 (last 2026-03-25); the owner's recent repos are forks and dotfiles; 0 x402scan payments to the payee in the window; the site still loads. [repo](https://github.com/markeljan/x420), [x402scan payee](https://www.x402scan.com/recipient/0x93109d14f6665a9c9675290d3cd5a6c07bbafdbd)
**Direction:** None stated. **Competitors:** Clanker itself, Autolaunch, SpeedRun, the Heurist machine below, and one-off x402 mint contracts (opinion).
**Green and red flags:** Green: working open repo; the token description says it has no utility. Red: pseudonymous operator; no payments since 2025-11-12; repo idle since March; fee split not stated; test-like entries in the 78-token list. Not confirmed: the full creator wallet, because the Clanker page was truncated and later blocked for the sub-agent.
**Verdict:** Speculative; momentum Stalled. Opinion: a quick pseudonymous launchpad built for the narrative moment. Its payment flow faded within three weeks and the code has been idle since March, though the site and token still exist.
**Tracking card:** Website x420.dev · docs none · GitHub markeljan/x420 (also microchipgnu) · X @x420yo (project), soko_eth (declared on markeljan's profile, link to project unconfirmed) · Farcaster soko.eth · Telegram not found · token 0x68e284F21D04D4D62398C290EC3EF41C97F80b07 · x402 payee 0x93109d14f6665a9c9675290d3cd5a6c07bbafdbd

#### Profile: x402 token (x402.team)
**One-line:** A pseudonymous Base meme token, "fair launch", whose site has no team, roadmap or social links. [x402.team](https://www.x402.team)
**x402 role:** None in the product sense. The token has a 1 USDC pay-to-mint contract: `mint` takes 1 USDC and sends 500,000 tokens to the chosen address. The USDC goes to a fee address set by the owner, who can also change the mint amount and fee address. [Sourcify source](https://sourcify.dev/server/v2/contract/8453/0xc3e9c4e09f9d418d64b20d3df155d6407dd0df87?fields=sources)
**Chains:** Base.
**Token:**
- x402, Base 0xbb101d25F2eaaE7C9AB877ecd64ef6d95c803b07, created 2025-10-25 08:38:05 UTC through the Clanker v4 factory by 0xe8713fcd...8f0. The Clanker page lists that wallet as creator, admin and reward recipient and shows no description. [Clanker page](https://www.clanker.world/clanker/0xbb101d25F2eaaE7C9AB877ecd64ef6d95c803b07)
- The mint contract (0xc3e9c4e0..., created 2025-10-25 09:18:59 UTC by the same wallet) still holds 9,000,000 tokens, enough for about 18 more mints (Base RPC read). The number of mints made was not found because log queries were blocked on the public RPCs. [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0xc3e9c4e09f9d418d64b20d3df155d6407dd0df87)
- The site says an error left about 1% of tokens temporarily unmintable, and promises an airdrop to people who post the contract address and a Base address. [x402.team](https://www.x402.team)
- Liveness: liquidity about $181k, 24h volume about $8 (2026-10-11). [DexScreener](https://dexscreener.com/base/0x9fdfb407dfd7d44e2b23bc06bebbc67dc6edf83933bdef0c4659ac9ff8683a96)
- No utility is stated.

**Team:** Anonymous. The creator wallet has EIP-7702 delegation code and nonce 246. It is not tied to any person. [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0xe8713fcdbf9324122030449974995fa1f93de8f0)
**Track record and pivots:** One event on 2025-10-25. Two minutes later a different wallet (0x00016e4f...) deployed a separate token also named x402 (0x4c15cf7c..., 1B supply). No deployer link between the two was found. [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/address/0x4c15cf7ccd50964ce823d204b34fff1c265e2633)
**Trend-hopping score (1-5):** 5. Narrative-only meme branding. [x402.team](https://www.x402.team)
**Funding:** None. **Product and traction:** No product. The x402scan pull has no payments to the mint contract, but x402scan only sees registered facilitators, so this is not proof of zero mints. **Last six months:** No site or code change found. **Direction:** None stated. **Competitors:** Not applicable (a meme).
**Green and red flags:** Green: the mint contract source is verified; the deployer holds none of the token. Red: anonymous team; owner-changeable mint amount and fee address; airdrop bait on the site; a same-day namesake token.
**Verdict:** Speculative; momentum Dead. Opinion: a meme with no team.

#### Profile: Covalent SpeedRun (Covalent / CXT)
**One-line:** SpeedRun was a prompt-to-app builder on Base from the data company Covalent, announced 2025-10-30 with Clanker token launches and x402 payments. On 2026-10-11 its site shows a maintenance notice. [launch post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/speedrun-is-going-live-today-with-clanker-and-x402-integration/), [speedrunapp.com](https://speedrunapp.com)
**x402 role:** Apps built in SpeedRun could take x402 micro-payments, and tokens could launch on Clanker. Covalent's own GoldRush x402 data proxy runs only on Base Sepolia testnet; mainnet is listed as upcoming with no date. [launch post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/speedrun-is-going-live-today-with-clanker-and-x402-integration/), [GoldRush x402 docs](https://goldrush.dev/docs/goldrush-x402/overview)
**Chains:** Base (SpeedRun, Base Mini Apps from 2025-12-15), Ethereum (CXT, staking), Moonbeam (legacy settlement). Covalent's pages state data coverage as 100+, 150+ and 230+ chains. [Dec 2025 post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/speedrun-is-going-live-with-base-mini-apps), [tokenomics](https://www.covalenthq.com/docs/cxt/tokenomics)
**Token:**
- CXT. Ethereum 0x7ABc8A5768E6bE61A6c693a6e4EAcb5B60602C4D (official). [tokenomics](https://www.covalenthq.com/docs/cxt/tokenomics)
- Base: 0xB1E1f3Cc2B6fE4420C1Ac82022b457018Eb628ff, a bridged token created 2024-11-22 04:42 UTC pointing at the Ethereum CXT through the standard Base bridge. Total supply on Base is about 9.92M of 1B (Base RPC read). [BaseScan](https://basescan.org/token/0xb1e1f3cc2b6fe4420c1ac82022b457018eb628ff)
- An Aerodrome pool on Base was created 2024-12-11 with about $27.9k liquidity (2026-10-11). The docs list the pool but not the Base token address. [DexScreener](https://dexscreener.com/base/0x923bebd9fbcaf0f85251a3d5a11bf5bfaa19942f)
- Old CQT is marked deprecated in Covalent's staking docs; the swap date and ratio were not found. The CXT contract repo was created 2024-03-20. [staking docs](https://www.covalenthq.com/docs/cxt/staking-guide), [repo](https://github.com/covalenthq/covalent-x-token)
- Stated utility: staking bond for node operators, governance, and future settlement. Supply is 1B, all minted at genesis. About 17M CXT were bought back off-chain in 2025 and about 9.05M via $1,000-a-day on-chain buys; a Strategic Reserve was announced 2025-10-23. [llms.txt](https://www.covalenthq.com/llms.txt), [reserve post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/introducing-the-covalent-strategic-reserve)
- It does real work in staking (homepage text shows 278.7M staked and 23 operators); API payments in CXT are described as a future mechanism. Covalent's pages disagree on circulation: 89.89% on the homepage text versus over 95% (Q2 2026) in the tokenomics doc. [llms.txt](https://www.covalenthq.com/llms.txt), [tokenomics](https://www.covalenthq.com/docs/cxt/tokenomics)

**Team (published by Covalent):** Ganesh Swami (co-founder, CEO), Levi Aul (co-founder, CTO), Arjun Kalsy (Chief of Growth), Adrian Jonklaas (CFO and COO), Harish Raisinghani (Developer Relations and API Business Manager), Rodrigo Garcia (Head of Platform Engineering), Pranay Valson (Head of Protocol Engineering), Rohit Tandon (Head of Design). Doxxed. The launch post is bylined Jayen Harill. X @Covalent_HQ is set on the GitHub org and linked from the site. Founders' personal handles were not looked up. [Covalent text export](https://www.covalenthq.com/llms-full.txt), [launch post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/speedrun-is-going-live-today-with-clanker-and-x402-integration/), [org](https://github.com/covalenthq)
**Track record and pivots:**
- 2017-11-22: GitHub org created. Covalent's pages give the founding year as 2017 and 2018, after winning a 2017 hackathon. [org](https://github.com/covalenthq), [llms.txt](https://www.covalenthq.com/llms.txt)
- 2024: rebrand of the API to GoldRush. The post's byline shows 2026-09-16, which conflicts with its 2024 related links, so that date is treated as unreliable. [GoldRush post](https://goldrush.dev/blog/embracing-a-new-era-introducing-goldrush-from-covalent/)
- 2024-12-16: AI agent SDK repo (121 stars, archived, last push 2025-12-02). 2025-03-15: MCP server repo (archived). [org](https://github.com/covalenthq)
- Q3 2025: SpeedRun beta, with XP now and CXT rewards later (Q3 recap, 2025-10-06). 2025-10-30: general launch with Clanker and x402. [recap](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/covalent-q3-2025-accelerating-the-cxt-flywheel)
- 2025-12-09: governance proposal to prepare moving network operations to Base (staking stays on Ethereum). 2025-12-15: SpeedRun adds Base Mini Apps. [proposal](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/governance-proposal-preparation-to-transition-the-covalent-network-to-base), [Mini Apps post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/speedrun-is-going-live-with-base-mini-apps)
- 2026-01-21: the year-end update (newsletter dated 2025-12-19) calls SpeedRun an inference platform and the first step of a new direction. [post](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/covalent-2025-year-end-a-holiday-update)
- 2026-02-17: GoldRush x402 (testnet), then CLI (2026-02-25) and Skills (2026-03-04). [changelog](https://goldrush.dev/docs/changelog)
- What happened to SpeedRun: the last mention found in Covalent posts is 2026-01-21; about a dozen of the roughly 25 posts since then were opened and none mention it. The Covalent products page now describes "Speedrun" as a sub-second data product beside GoldRush. The app site shows maintenance, and the reason is not found. [products](https://www.covalenthq.com/products), [speedrunapp.com](https://speedrunapp.com)

**Trend-hopping score (1-5):** 2 for Covalent, a data-infrastructure company since 2017 whose agent, x402 and vibe-coding products extend that theme. SpeedRun alone would score 3: a consumer app timed to the Clanker and x402 moment, now paused.
**Funding:** Backers named on Covalent's own pages: Binance Labs, Coinbase Ventures, 1kx, Delphi Digital, Woodstock, Maelstrom, Rocktree. The GoldRush post claims $5M in strategic funding for APAC expansion, with the unreliable date noted above. Other round sizes were not found. [ecosystem export](https://www.covalenthq.com/llms-full.txt), [GoldRush post](https://goldrush.dev/blog/embracing-a-new-era-introducing-goldrush-from-covalent/)
**Product and traction:** GoldRush covers 100+ chains, and customers named on its pages include EY, Fidelity, Consensys and Chainalysis. SpeedRun: no app, user or volume numbers found; the Q3 recap and year-end note say early builders shipped dashboards, games and experiments, and a WallChain campaign carries $200K in CXT over 5 months. Not confirmed: whether any Clanker token was launched through SpeedRun. [ecosystem export](https://www.covalenthq.com/llms-full.txt), [recap](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/covalent-q3-2025-accelerating-the-cxt-flywheel)
**Last six months (2026-04-11 to 2026-10-11):** Covalent's own repos had 28 commits in total, all in May 2026 (refiner 5, bsp-agent 10, ewm-das 4, homebrew-covalent 7, orbital 2) and none since 2026-05-22; the ccxt repo's June push is an upstream fork, so it is not Covalent code; goldrush-agent-skills last pushed 2026-03-09 ([org](https://github.com/covalenthq)). GoldRush shipped Hyperliquid streams (many entries Apr to Sep), the Pipeline API (2026-05-13) and an L4 order book stream (2026-09-30); the latest blog post is dated 2026-09-24. [changelog](https://goldrush.dev/docs/changelog)
**Direction:** Agents, x402 on mainnet (undated), Hyperliquid data, and the network's move to Base. [changelog](https://goldrush.dev/docs/changelog), [proposal](https://www.covalenthq.com/blog/governance-proposal-preparation-to-transition-the-covalent-network-to-base)
**Competitors:** Opinion, though Covalent publishes migration and compare pages for Alchemy, Zerion, DeBank, Dune Sim and Moralis; for SpeedRun, other prompt-to-app builders and Clanker-based launchers. [GoldRush index](https://goldrush.dev/llms.txt)
**Green and red flags:** Green: named team, long-running business, enterprise customers, open protocol docs, honest changelog. Red: SpeedRun is down; x402 is testnet-only; public code is quiet since May; mixed supply figures; a date glitch on a funding claim.
**Verdict:** Likely abandoned for SpeedRun; the parent Covalent is a Serious builder. Momentum Stalled for SpeedRun, Steady for GoldRush. Opinion: the company is real and still shipping data features, but the launch tool that tied it to x402 and Clanker has gone quiet since January.

#### Profile: Quick Intel "Tator" (x402.quickintel.io)
**One-line:** Quick Intel is a token security-scanner company. Its x402 gateway sells the scanner at $0.03 a call and its Tator trading agent at $0.20 a call. Token launching on Clanker and Pump.fun is a Tator capability described in its skills repo, not a separate product. [gateway](https://x402.quickintel.io), [skills repo](https://github.com/Quick-Intel/openclaw-skills)
**x402 role:**
- The gateway reported healthy on 2026-10-11 (version 1.5.0). It runs x402 v2 and a second payment protocol, with self-settlement. No paid call was made, so a working end-to-end launch is not confirmed. [health](https://x402.quickintel.io/health), [discovery](https://x402.quickintel.io/accepted)
- The gateway's own description of Tator lists swaps, bridging, limit orders, DCA, perps and prediction markets, and returns unsigned transactions. It does not mention token launching. [discovery](https://x402.quickintel.io/accepted)
- The skills repo lists token launching on Clanker (Base) and Pump.fun (Solana). It does not mention Flaunch. The fee split is 90% creator and 10% Tator of the 1% pool fee, plus a 0.2% Clanker protocol fee. [skills repo](https://github.com/Quick-Intel/openclaw-skills)
- A third-party listing cited in the brief (termo.ai) returns 404, and its "10% interface fee" and "Flaunch" claims are not supported by the official sources above. [termo.ai](https://termo.ai/skills/token-launch-pad)
- x402scan shows no recorded Base payments to either payTo address (queried 2026-10-11); the gateway settles locally, so x402scan may not see them. [scan payTo](https://basescan.org/address/0x3dBDfB6E5dCFa8f51AA07bC3aDf18a62b186C362), [Tator payTo](https://basescan.org/address/0x6a35dF48189eB83404d97b32dd3E91517ff4ceb9)

**Chains:** Payment on Base plus 13 other EVM networks and Solana; the scanner covers 63 chains and Tator 24. [gateway](https://x402.quickintel.io), [discovery](https://x402.quickintel.io/accepted)
**Token:** QKNTL, a utility token on Ethereum and Arbitrum with tiers and benefits. It has no Base token, and no contract address, launch date or method was found in the docs pages read. Tator's consumer-app fee reductions use a points scheme called Tots. [token docs](https://docs.quickintel.io/qkntl-token/token-information.md), [Tator page](https://quickintel.io/tator-trader)
**Team:** No individuals named on any official page; the company is described as a team. X @quickintel_ai is set on the GitHub org; X @quickaboricua is linked from the gateway page without explanation; @tator_trader and Farcaster tator-onchain come from the official links page. The ClawHub skill publisher is azep-ninja (a pseudonymous handle on the gateway page). Pseudonymous or anonymous. [org](https://github.com/Quick-Intel), [gateway](https://x402.quickintel.io), [links](https://docs.quickintel.io/resources/official-tator-trader-links.md)
**Track record and pivots:** GitHub org created 2023-03-21. Repo dates: 2023-09 MetaMask Snap; 2023-11 contracts; 2024-01 API monetization; 2025-02 agent studio contracts; 2026-02-13 Tator repo; 2026-02-19 OpenClaw skills. The gateway lists agent IDs #18038 and #18132 in the ERC-8004 registry. [org](https://github.com/Quick-Intel), [gateway](https://x402.quickintel.io)
**Trend-hopping score (1-5):** 2. A security scanner since 2023 that adds agent and x402 channels while keeping the core product. [org](https://github.com/Quick-Intel)
**Funding:** Not found. **Product and traction:** Gateway live and healthy. The skills repo has 0 stars and 10 commits. No usage numbers found. [skills repo](https://github.com/Quick-Intel/openclaw-skills)
**Last six months (2026-04-11 to 2026-10-11):** GitHub: docs repo 1 commit (Oct 2026), .github 2 (Apr 2026), tator-trader and skills 0. The gateway is live at v1.5.0. [org](https://github.com/Quick-Intel), [health](https://x402.quickintel.io/health)
**Direction:** More chains and agent channels; no roadmap found. **Competitors:** Other token scanners and trading-agent APIs (opinion).
**Green and red flags:** Green: working discovery and health endpoints; consistent product line. Red: no named team; no usage data; launch capability documented only in a skills repo; the Tator persona's page names no one. Not confirmed: that launching works through the paid API.
**Verdict:** Credible but small; momentum Steady. Opinion: a security vendor adding x402 as a sales channel. Token launching here is a minor feature, not the business.
